\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}
, if
u
and
v
were YZ-measured
before the pivot, they will be XY-measured afterwards.

Lemma C.4

(
[
15
, Lemma B.9]
)
.

Given a graph
G=(V,E)
,
A\subseteq V
,
u\in V
, and
v\in N_{G}(u)
,

\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(A\right)=\begin{cases}\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(A\right)&\text{if }u,v\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[A\right]\\
\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(A\right)\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}[v]&\text{if }u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[A\right],v\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[A\right]\\
\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(A\right)\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}[u]&\text{if }u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[A\right],v\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[A\right]\\
\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(A\right)\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}[u]\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}[v]&\text{if }u,v\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[A\right].\end{cases}

Proof of Lemma
5.3
.

First, note (e.g. via Remark
C.3
) that
u,v\in N_{G}[u]
, that symmetrically
u,v\in N_{G}[v]
, and that

\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[A\mathbin{\Delta}\{u\}\right]=\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[A\right]\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}[u].

Thus, for any set
A\subseteq V
, we have
u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[A\mathbin{\Delta}\{u\}\right]\Leftrightarrow u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[A\right]
and similarly
v\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[A\mathbin{\Delta}\{u\}\right]\Leftrightarrow v\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[A\right]
.

By Lemma
C.4
, in the four cases (depending on which of
u,v
are in
\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]
), we have:

•

If
u,v\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]
, then

\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c^{\prime}(w)\right)=\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c(w)\right)=\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)

•

If
u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]
and
v\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]
, then
u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{u\}\right]
and
v\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{u\}\right]
; thus

\displaystyle\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c^{\prime}(w)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{u\}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{u\}\right)\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}[u]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}(u)\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}[u]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)\mathbin{\Delta}\{u\}

•

If
u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right],v\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]
, then symmetrically to the previous case,

\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c^{\prime}(w)\right)=\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{v\}\right)=\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)\mathbin{\Delta}\{v\}

•

If
u,v\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]
, then
u,v\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{u,v\}\right]
and thus

\displaystyle\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c^{\prime}(w)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{u,v\}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{u,v\}\right)\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}[u]\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}[v]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}(u)\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}(v)\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}[u]\mathbin{\Delta}N_{G}[v]

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)\mathbin{\Delta}\{u,v\}

These results are summarised in Table
1
.
They straightforwardly imply
\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left[c^{\prime}(w)\right]=\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]
for all
w\in\bar{O}
: in each row, the changes to correction set and odd neighbourhood are the same, so they cancel out when computing the closed odd neighbourhood.

Note that the only changes to correction sets and odd neighbourhoods are symmetric differences with some subset of
\{u,v\}
, so Pauli flow and (where applicable) focusing conditions involving only vertices other than
u,v
remain satisfied.

Case

c^{\prime}(w)

\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c^{\prime}(w)\right)

u,v\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]

c(w)

\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)

u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right],v\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]

c(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{u\}

\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)\mathbin{\Delta}\{u\}

u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right],v\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]

c(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{v\}

\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)\mathbin{\Delta}\{v\}

u,v\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]

c(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{u,v\}

\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)\mathbin{\Delta}\{u,v\}

Table 1:
Correction sets and odd neighbourhoods after pivoting in Lemma
5.3
, depending on the relationship of
u
and
v
to the original closed odd neighbourhood.

Additionally, whether a symmetric difference is taken with
u
(and what effect this may have) is independent of what is done regarding
v
.
By symmetry, it thus suffices to consider only
u
.
First, consider the correction set for
u
itself.

•

If
\lambda(u)=XY
, then
u\notin c(u)\wedge u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(u)\right)
by
(P4)
, so
u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(u)\right]
.
Thus we take symmetric differences with
u
, which means
u\in c^{\prime}(u)\wedge u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c^{\prime}(u)\right)
.
Yet note that
\lambda^{\prime}(u)=YZ
and the above is exactly what is needed to satisfy
(P6)
.

•

The case
\lambda(u)=YZ
is symmetric to
\lambda(u)=XY
.

•

If
\lambda(u)=XZ
, then
u\in c(u)\wedge u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(u)\right)
, so
u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(u)\right]
.
Thus the membership of
u
in those sets does not change and
(P5)
remains satisfied.

•

If
\lambda(u)=X
, then
u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(u)\right)
by
(P7)
, and
\lambda^{\prime}(u)=Z
.
There are two subcases:

–

If furthermore
u\in c(u)
, then
u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(u)\right]
and nothing changes, meaning
u\in c^{\prime}(u)
and
u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c^{\prime}(u)\right)
.
Hence
(P9)
is satisfied.

–

If instead
u\notin c(u)
, then
u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(u)\right]
.
Thus symmetric differences with
u
are taken and we have
u\in c^{\prime}(u)
and
u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c(u^{\prime})\right)
.
Again,
(P9)
is satisfied.

•

The case
\lambda(u)=Z
is symmetric to
\lambda(u)=X
.

•

If
\lambda(u)=Y
, then there are two possibilities but in either case
u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(u)\right]
by
(P8)
.
Thus symmetric differences are taken, and afterwards
u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left[c^{\prime}(u)\right]
again.
The measurement label remains
\lambda^{\prime}(u)=Y
and
(P8)
remains satisfied.

Now suppose
w\in\bar{O}\setminus\{u\}
.
Note that by the first part of the proof,

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:pivot-changes}u\in c^{\prime}(w)\Leftrightarrow u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)\quad\text{and}\quad u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c^{\prime}(w)\right)\Leftrightarrow u\in c(w).

(3)

If
u\notin c(w)
and
u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)
, then analogous properties will hold after pivoting, so there is nothing to prove.
We will therefore only consider cases where
u
is in the correction set, the odd neighbourhood, or both.

•

If
\lambda(u)=XY
, and
u
appears in
c(w)
and/or
\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)
, then
w\prec u
by
(P1)
and
(P2)
.
So, whenever
u
appears in
c^{\prime}(w)
and/or
\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c^{\prime}(w)\right)
, we have
w\prec u
and thus
(P1)
and
(P2)
remain satisfied for
c^{\prime}(w)
.

•

The case
\lambda(u)=YZ
is analogous to
\lambda(u)=XY
.

•

The case
\lambda(u)=XZ
is also analogous to
\lambda(u)=XY
.

•

If
\lambda(u)=X
, there are two subcases.

–

Suppose
u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)
, then
w\prec u
.
Then
u\in c^{\prime}(w)
but
(P1)
remains satisfied after the change of label to
\lambda^{\prime}(u)=Z
since we still have
w\prec u
.

–

Suppose
u\in c(w)
and
u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)
.
In this case, nothing is implied about the order of
u
and
w
.
After the pivot, we have
\lambda^{\prime}(u)=Z
,
u\notin c^{\prime}(w)
and
u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G\wedge uv}\left(c^{\prime}(w)\right)
.
Thus,
(P2)
is satisfied.

•

The case
\lambda(u)=Z
is symmetric to
\lambda(u)=X
.

•

If
\lambda(u)=Y
, there are again two cases.

–

If
u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]
, then we know nothing about the order of
u
and
w
because of
(P3)
, but nothing changes regarding the correction set or the label of
u
, so
(P3)
remains satisfied.

–

If
u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]
, then by
(P3)
, we have
w\prec u
.
In this case
u
moves from the correction set to the odd neighbourhood, or conversely, but
(P3)
remains satisfied.

The arguments regarding
v
are independent of and analogous to the ones for
u
, so this completes the proof that
(p^{\prime},\prec)
is a Pauli flow.

For focusing, it suffices to consider the correction sets of vertices other than
u
(as focusing does not talk about the relationship between a vertex and its own correction set or the latter’s odd neighbourhood).
So suppose
w\in\bar{O}\setminus\{u\}
.

•

If
\lambda(u)=Y
then
(p,\prec)
being focused implies
u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left[c(w)\right]
via
(F3)
.
But then nothing changes during the pivot, not even the measurement label of
u
, so
(F3)
remains satisfied.

•

If
\lambda(u)=XY
or
\lambda(u)=X
, then
(p,\prec)
being focused implies
u\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)
via
(F2)
.
After the pivot,
\lambda^{\prime}(u)\in\{YZ,Z\}
and
u\notin{c^{\prime}(w)}
by (
3
), so
(F1)
is satisfied.

•

If
\lambda(u)=YZ
or
\lambda(u)=Z
, the argument is symmetric to the previous case.

•

The case
\lambda(u)=XZ
is excluded.

Thus
(p,\prec)
being focused implies that
(p^{\prime},\prec)
is focused, assuming the vertices incident on the pivot edge are not
XZ
-measured.

Finally, since the gflow conditions are a subset of the Pauli flow conditions and pivoting maps planar measurements to planar measurements, if
(p,\prec)
is a gflow, then
(p^{\prime},\prec^{\prime})
is also a gflow.
∎

Remark C.5
.

In Lemma
5.3
, focusing may break if one of the vertices incident on the pivot edge is
XZ
-measured, say
\lambda(u)=XZ
: assume
(p,\prec)
is focused, then we know
u\notin c(w)
for any
w\neq u
but we may have
u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)
.
In the latter case, we would have
u\in c^{\prime}(w)
after the pivot but
\lambda(u)=XZ
still, so the flow is no longer focused.
Of course it can be re-focused by replacing
c^{\prime}(w)
with
c^{\prime}(w)\mathbin{\Delta}c^{\prime}(u)
for all
w
such that
u\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(w)\right)
, but the straightforward modification of Lemma
5.3
is no longer sufficient.

Proof of Theorem
5.5
.

By Lemma
C.2
, the vertex splitting operation of Definition
5.4
can be expressed as a sequence of two
YZ
-insertions and a pivot, as well as an operation on measurement angles that does not affect the labelled open graphs.
As
\left|I\right|=\left|O\right|
, the focused Pauli flow on each labelled open graph is unique.
Using the same numbering as in the above lemma,
\Gamma_{1}
has Pauli flow if and only if
\Gamma
has Pauli flow by Corollary
4.6
.
Similarly, by Lemma
5.3
and the property that pivoting is involutive,
\Gamma_{4}
has Pauli flow if and only if
\Gamma_{3}
has Pauli flow.
In each case, there are no conditions on the flows.
Thus it remains only to show that step 3 – the second
YZ
-insertion – preserves the existence of Pauli flow under the given conditions.

First, suppose
\Gamma
has focused Pauli flow
(c,\prec)
where without loss of generality
\prec
is the induced partial order.
Then, by Corollary
4.6
, the unique focused Pauli flow on
\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma_{1}
satisfies

{\prec_{2}}={\prec}\cup\{(w,x^{\prime\prime})\mid w\prec x\}\cup\{(x^{\prime\prime},v)\mid x\prec v\}\quad\text{and}\quad c_{2}(v)=\begin{cases}c(x)\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\}&\text{if }v=x^{\prime\prime}\\
c(v)&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Furthermore, by Theorem
4.3
,
\Gamma_{3}
has focused Pauli flow if and only if there exists
K\subseteq V_{2}=V\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\}
such that three conditions hold with respect to the neighbourhood
S=W\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\}
of the new vertex.
We now show those conditions follow from
(V1)
–
(V4)
.

•

The only difference between
\Gamma_{2}
and
\Gamma
is the insertion of the
YZ
-measured vertex
x^{\prime\prime}
with a single edge to
x
.
Thus
K
being focused over
\bar{O}\setminus(W\cap\mathcal{X})
in
\Gamma
by
(V1)
implies
K
being focused over
(V\setminus O)\setminus(W\cap\mathcal{X}_{2})
in
\Gamma_{2}
.

Note that
x^{\prime\prime}\notin K
by assumption, so
K
is focused over
\{x^{\prime\prime}\}
.
Therefore by Observation
B.1
,
K
is focused over
(V\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\}\setminus O)\setminus(W\cap\mathcal{X}_{2})
in
\Gamma_{2}
.
We can also add
x^{\prime\prime}
to
W
since
x^{\prime\prime}\notin\mathcal{X}_{2}
.
Hence
K
is focused over
\bar{O}_{2}\setminus((W\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\})\cap\mathcal{X}_{2})
, which is the first part of condition
1
.
The second part follows similarly from
(V1)
.

•

We have
\left|W\cap K\right|\equiv 0\bmod 2
by
(V1)
and
x^{\prime\prime}\notin K
.
Thus
\left|(W\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\})\cap K\right|\equiv 0\bmod 2
, which is condition
2
.

•

Denote by
\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
and
\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
the sets from condition
3
, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\left\{w\in\bar{O}_{2}:\left|c(w)\cap(W\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\})\right|\equiv 1\bmod 2\right\}

(4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{2}\cap(K\cup(\mathsf{Odd}_{G_{2}}\left(K\right)\mathbin{\Delta}(W\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\})))

(5)

Note that
x^{\prime\prime}
does not appear in any correction set other than its own because of focusing, so the first definition reduces to

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:Wpred}\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}=\begin{cases}\mathcal{W}_{pred}&\text{if }\left|c(x)\cap W\right|\equiv 1\bmod 2\\
\mathcal{W}_{pred}\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\}&\text{if }\left|c(x)\cap W\right|\equiv 0\bmod 2\end{cases}

(6)

Similarly, since
x^{\prime\prime}\notin K
, the set
\mathsf{Odd}_{G_{2}}\left(K\right)
is equal to
\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(K\right)
if
x\notin K
and to
\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(K\right)\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\}
if
x\in K
.
Thus:

\addcontentsline{lla}{section}{\numberline q:Vsucc}\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}=\begin{cases}\mathcal{V}_{succ}&\text{if }x\in K\\
\mathcal{V}_{succ}\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\}&\text{if }x\notin K\end{cases}

(7)

We distinguish cases depending on the parity of
\left|c(x)\cap W\right|
and on whether
x
is in
K
.

–

Suppose
\left|c(x)\cap W\right|\equiv 1\bmod 2
and
x\in K
.
This is forbidden by
(V4)
so can be ignored.

–

Suppose
\left|c(x)\cap W\right|\equiv 0\bmod 2
and
x\in K
.
Take
w\in\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
.

If
w=x^{\prime\prime}
, then for all
u\in V
such that
u\prec_{2}x^{\prime\prime}
we also have
u\prec x
by the definition of
\prec_{2}
, and thus by
(V3)

u\notin\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
.
Moreover,
x^{\prime\prime}\notin\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
since
x\in K
.
Thus
\neg(v\prec_{2}w\vee v=w)
for all
v\in\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
.

If
w\neq x^{\prime\prime}
, the result follows from
(V2)
, noting that for all
u,v\in V
we have
u\prec_{2}v
if and only if
u\prec v
.

–

Suppose
\left|c(x)\cap W\right|\equiv 1\bmod 2
and
x\notin K
.
This is again forbidden by
(V4)
.

–

Suppose
\left|c(x)\cap W\right|\equiv 0\bmod 2
and
x\notin K
.
Take
v\in\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
.

If
v=x^{\prime\prime}
, then for all
u\in V
such that
x^{\prime\prime}\prec_{2}u
we also have
x\prec u
by the definition of
\prec_{2}
, and thus by
(V3)

u\notin\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
.
Moreover,
x^{\prime\prime}\notin\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
since
\left|c(x)\cap W\right|\equiv 0\bmod 2
.
Thus
\neg(v\prec_{2}w\vee v=w)
for all
w\in\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
.

If
v\neq x^{\prime\prime}
, the result again follows from
(V2)
and noting that for all
u,w\in V
we have
u\prec_{2}w
if and only if
u\prec w
.

Thus condition
3
follows from
(V2)
–
(V4)
.

We have shown that
\Gamma^{\prime}
has Pauli flow if
\Gamma
has a focused Pauli flow and we can find a set
K
such that
(V1)
–
(V4)
hold.

For the reverse direction, assume
\Gamma^{\prime}
has focused Pauli flow
(c_{4},\prec_{4})
, then by Lemma
5.3
,
\Gamma_{3}
has focused Pauli flow
(c_{3},\prec_{3})
, where
{\prec_{3}}={\prec_{4}}
and for all
w\in V_{4}=V\cup\{x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\}
:

c_{3}(w):=\begin{cases}c_{4}(w)&\text{if }x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G_{4}}\left[c_{4}(w)\right]\\
c_{4}(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{x^{\prime}\}&\text{if }x^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G_{4}}\left[c_{4}(w)\right],x^{\prime\prime}\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G_{4}}\left[c_{4}(w)\right]\\
c_{4}(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{x^{\prime\prime}\}&\text{if }x^{\prime}\notin\mathsf{Odd}_{G_{4}}\left[c_{4}(w)\right],x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G_{4}}\left[c_{4}(w)\right]\\
c_{4}(w)\mathbin{\Delta}\{x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\}&\text{if }x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{Odd}_{G_{4}}\left[c_{4}(w)\right]\end{cases}

Take
K^{\prime}=c_{3}(x^{\prime})\setminus\{x^{\prime}\}
, i.e.
K^{\prime}=c_{4}(x^{\prime})\setminus\{x^{\prime\prime}\}
if
x^{\prime\prime}\in c_{4}(x^{\prime})
and
K^{\prime}=c_{4}(x^{\prime})
otherwise.
By Theorem
4.3
, we know that
\Gamma_{2}
(and thus iteratively
\Gamma
) has Pauli flow, and conditions
1
–
3
hold for
\Gamma_{2}
.

1.

K^{\prime}
being focused over
\bar{O}_{2}\setminus((W\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\})\cap\mathcal{X}_{2})
and no larger set implies
K^{\prime}
is focused over
(\bar{O}\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\})\setminus(W\cap\mathcal{X})
since
\lambda_{2}(x^{\prime\prime})=YZ
.
Now
x^{\prime\prime}\notin K^{\prime}
by definition, so
(F1)
–
(F3)
hold with respect to
x^{\prime\prime}
and we may limit attention to the remaining vertices.
This yields the condition of
K^{\prime}
being focused over
\bar{O}\setminus(W\cap\mathcal{X})
and no larger set, which is exactly the definition of
K
.
Moreover,
\left|(W\cup\{x^{\prime\prime}\})\cap K^{\prime}\right|\equiv 0\bmod 2
implies
\left|W\cap K^{\prime}\right|\equiv 0\bmod 2
since
x^{\prime\prime}\notin K^{\prime}
by the definition above.
Thus
(V1)
holds.

2.

Let
\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
and
\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
as defined in (
4
) and(
5
).
Then all
w\in\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
and all
v\in\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
satisfying
\neg(v\prec_{2}w\vee v=w)
immediately implies
(V2)
via (
6
) and (
7
), as well as noting that for vertices
u,u^{\prime}\in V
, we have
u\prec_{2}u^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow u\prec u^{\prime}
.

Then from (
6
), we see that
x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
if and only if
\left|c(x)\cap W\right|\equiv 0\bmod 2
and
x\in\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
if and only if
\left|c(x)\cap W\right|\equiv 1\bmod 2
.
Similarly, from (
7
), we deduce that
x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
if and only if
x\notin K
and
x\in\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
if and only if
x\in K
.
Thus the requirement that
\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
and
\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
do not share elements implies either
\left|c(x)\cap W\right|\equiv 0\bmod 2
and
x\in K
, or
\left|c(x)\cap W\right|\equiv 1\bmod 2
and
x\notin K
: i.e.
(V4)
holds.
It also means that exactly one of
x,x^{\prime\prime}
is in
\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
and the other is in
\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
.

Finally, by the uniqueness of focused Pauli flow on labelled open graphs with
\left|I\right|=\left|O\right|
and by Corollary
4.6
, we know that
x
and
x^{\prime\prime}
must have the same partial order relationships in
\prec_{2}
.
Suppose
x\in\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
and
x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
(the other case is analogous).
Then for all
v\in\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
, condition
3
implies
\neg(v\prec_{2}x)
.
So if
u\in V
satisfies
u\prec x
and thus
u\prec_{2}x
, then
u\notin\mathcal{V}_{succ}^{(2)}
.
Similarly, for all
w\in\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
, condition
3
implies
\neg(v\prec_{2}x^{\prime\prime})
.
So if
u\in V
satisfies
x\prec u
and thus
x^{\prime\prime}\prec_{2}u
, then
u\notin\mathcal{W}_{pred}^{(2)}
.
Thus
(V3)
holds.

This establishes all six conditions hold if
\Gamma^{\prime}
has Pauli flow.
∎

Proof of Corollary
5.6
.

Suppose
\Gamma
has a focused Pauli flow
(c,\prec)
which satisfies the two conditions and where without loss of generality
\prec
is the induced partial order.
Take
K:=c(b)
in Theorem
5.5
.
Then focusing of
(c,\prec)
implies
K
is focused over
\bar{O}\setminus\{b\}
and
(P4)
implies
K
is not focused over
\{b\}
as well as
\left|\{b\}\cap c(b)\right|=0\bmod 2\Leftrightarrow b\notin c(b)
.
Together these imply
(V1)
.
We also have

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{pred}:=\{w\in\bar{O}:\left|c(w)\cap\{b\}\right|\equiv 1\bmod 2\}=\{w\in\bar{O}\mid b\in c(w)\}=\{w\in\bar{O}\mid w\triangleleft_{c}b\}

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{succ}:=\mathcal{L}\cap(c(b)\cup(\mathsf{Odd}_{G}\left(c(b)\right)\mathbin{\Delta}\{b\}))=\{v\in\bar{O}\mid b\triangleleft_{c}v\}.

Thus
(V2)
follows directly from
\prec
being the induced partial order (cf. Definition
2.14
).
Next,
(V3)
reduces to
a\prec u\implies\neg(u\triangleleft_{c}b)
and
u\prec a\implies\neg(b\triangleleft_{c}u)
for all
u\in V
, which follows from the second bullet point.
Finally,
(V4)
becomes
a\in c(b)\Leftrightarrow b\notin c(a)
, which is equivalent to the first bullet point.
Thus all the conditions of Theorem
5.5
are satisfied and the operation preserves the existence of Pauli flow.

For the other direction, suppose
\Gamma^{\prime}
has Pauli flow.
Then by Theorem
5.5
,
\Gamma
has a focused Pauli flow
(c,\prec)
such that conditions
(V1)
–
(V4)
hold.
We may assume without loss of generality that
\prec
is the induced partial order (cf. Section
2.3
), since removing relationships from the partial order will not break any of the four conditions.
Then, from the above,
(V4)
implies the first bullet point.
Now suppose for a contradiction
u\in V
satisfies
b\prec u\prec a
.
Then there exists a sequence
u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}
such that
b\triangleleft_{c}u_{1}\triangleleft_{c}\ldots\triangleleft_{c}u_{n}\triangleleft_{c}u
since
\prec
is the induced partial order.
Thus there exists
u_{1}\in\mathcal{V}_{succ}
such that
u_{1}\prec a
, which contradicts condition
(V3)
so this cannot happen and we have
u\prec a\implies\neg(b\prec u)
.
A similar argument works for the case
a\prec u\prec b
.
Thus the second bullet point holds.
∎
